Focus governs camera, animation, principal speech or action, resolution
language, and possible Tale attribution. The player never selects or rerolls
Focus directly, and it grants no extra action, hidden contribution, success
guarantee, or protection from consequence. Chapter 1.5 and Appendix E provide
the complete Focus and Item references.


# PART V — AWAY: EXPEDITION AND RETURN

Part V begins when named Citizens leave Home and ends when Homecoming returns
their material, bodily, relational, and remembered consequences to the Colony.

**TRAVEL is the Away mirror of DWELL.** It is the open Register for guiding a
particular party through the continuous Field. RISK temporarily takes focus
when a Roadway must be crossed, while MEET presents consequential situations
wherever bounded choice is useful. Launch and Homecoming are dedicated MEETs
fitted to departure and return. Stopover is the separate locked sleep screen
used wherever the party commits to sleep.

## 5.1 Leaving Home

Leaving Home commits named Citizens to an unpredictable world. Expeditions may
answer domestic need, pursue exploration or contact, respond to a visible
world condition, or begin without a fixed objective. The player may change
direction, follow discovery, and return with a result unlike the one first
imagined.

Preparation exposes only facts the Colony could know: present Day Band,
weather, visible traffic, known terrain and passages, current bodily
conditions, Items, Carry, and the exact civic effect of departure. Unreached
Nodes, upcoming MEETs, ideal equipment, hidden requirements, and exact odds
remain undisclosed. Citizens prepare broadly rather than solving the journey
before it begins.

### The Launch MEET

The player initiates Launch from DWELL while no expedition is active. Every
mechanically eligible Citizen appears for direct selection; eligibility follows
only from Home presence, Maturity, Guest type, bodily availability, and current
commitment. Citizens neither volunteer nor refuse. The player chooses which
particular lives to expose, assigns one prepared Supply to each eligible carrier
if desired, reviews standing Tools and Keepsakes, and may revise or withdraw
before Commit fixes the party and begins TRAVEL.

An ordinary party contains one and one-half to three Body Units and at least
two actual animals. Rabbit, Squirrel, and each v0.5 Expedition Guest occupy one
Body Unit; Mouse occupies one-half. Party membership remains fixed until
Homecoming. Supply positions also remain limited to one each, but any position
that is empty at a usable Outpost may choose anew from the full prepared Colony
stock. The one active expedition begins TRAVEL through the Home Median.

### Home subtraction and elapsed contribution

Launch immediately changes selected Citizens from Home to Away. Their standing
Roles remain, but their Civic Shares, Practice support, passive throughput, and
Beautification contribution leave Home; Role Load and Residence ownership do
not. The Launch preview shows the exact resulting Role Balances, production,
and Project conflicts without forecasting later Home MEETs.

Time-based contribution accrues only during the Day Bands in which a Citizen
actually sustains the relevant Role. Each eligible Band contributes one-quarter
of the ordinary daily amount. Fractions remain unseen in their defined
accumulators until DAWN awards whole amounts and carries the remainder forward.
Homecoming restores eligible contribution from the Citizen's actual return.
Appendices E and G provide the full party, Item, subtraction, and partial-day
reference.

## 5.2 TRAVEL: The Field

TRAVEL directly drives one compact party figure through a top-down, persistent
world at animal scale. The party begins within the Home Median and may move
longitudinally through Reaches, cross smaller roads at Reach borders, cross Main
Roadways into the Margins, and approach the Sound Walls beyond them. Nodes,
Outposts, shelter, vegetation, drainage, debris, and human infrastructure
remain fixed within that geography.

> **DWELL asks:** What can this Colony sustain, and what should it become?
>
> **TRAVEL asks:** Where can this party go, and what will it encounter?

Soil, grass, roots, grade, cover, water, barriers, shoulders, culverts, litter,
weather, visibility, sound, and continuity of passage make a short human-scale
distance into substantial terrain. The Median offers relative sanctuary;
Margins offer greater abundance and disturbance; Roadways are moving barriers.
Return traverses this same world rather than selecting Home from a menu.

The party moves as one Field subject while every Citizen remains named and
inspectable. Movement, elapsed time, destination, Carry, and general burden are
party facts; bodies, Items, relationships, Wounds, Exposure, and personal
consequences remain individual. Separation requires an explicit consequence.

### Attention and time

Away remains under player operation until Homecoming. The player may inspect
Home through its information lenses, while construction, Role reassignment,
new Project commitment, and another Launch remain closed. Home's clock-bound
systems continue. A Home MEET may briefly take focus, advance shared time, and
then return operation to the party's exact persistent position; the party never
travels, activates a Node, crosses, or decides autonomously.

The Day Band is the sole unit of world time. Each Band begins with a projected
**travel span** expressed as reachable ground. Direct movement—including
detours and backtracking—consumes that span according to terrain, weather,
Cargo, bodily condition, Rabbit composition, and pushing. Pausing preserves
the exact position and remaining reach. Exhaustion advances the world to the
next Band; Night proceeds through DAWN accounting before Morning.

Brief uncontested Node use may resolve within the Band that reached it. Work
that genuinely occupies time advances the next Band, and consequential use
opens MEET. The game has no Time Mark, encounter allowance, or action-point
substitute for physical movement and situated commitments.

### Species, Carry, and range

Rabbit bodies increase normal travel span on the absolute three-Body-Unit
scale:

**Rabbit TRAVEL multiplier = 1 + (Rabbit Body Units ÷ 3)**

| Rabbit Body Units | Normal travel span |
|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1× |
| 1 | 1⅓× |
| 2 | 1⅔× |
| 3 | 2× |

The increase extends distance within the current Day Band. Non-Rabbit bodies
apply no slowest-member penalty, and underfilling the party preserves Home
Civic Shares without amplifying Rabbit contribution.

Carry is one visible party-wide capacity for fungible Cargo. Rabbit and
Squirrel contribute 10 secured slots per Body Unit; each Mouse contributes 6;
Expedition Guests use their fixed values. More Cargo contracts projected reach
and may become a situated stake. Squirrel can accept additional Strained Carry
and its Jostle risk under Appendix E. Personal Items occupy their own positions.
Ordinary food remains abstracted Away: travelers continue to count toward Home
Sustenance demand, while recovered food is Cargo for return rather than a ration
meter.

### Travel cadence, Stopover, and situated interruptions

A rested party receives three traveled Day Bands at normal reach, then may push
through three more:

| Traveled Band since Stopover | Projected reach |
|---:|---:|
| 1–3 | 1× |
| 4 | ¾× |
| 5 | ½× |
| 6 | ¼× |
| 7 | unavailable until Stopover |

A party may commit to **Stopover** wherever it can physically sleep. The
Stopover screen is a locked, static DWELL-like view of the party inhabiting
that exact ground for sleep, with informational overlays for Day accounting,
cadence, bodily state, and applicable place effects. Sleeping contains no
decision and is not MEET. Completion advances one Band, resets cadence, ends
Tharn, and stabilizes current Wounds through the next three traveled Bands and
their resolved situations.

Stopover at a usable Outpost, within the Ancestral Home, or within Rest-Stop
Metropolis is uninterrupted. Stopover in regular Field carries a chance of a
separate situated MEET during the sleeping interval. Ground, shelter, weather,
and disturbance shape that possible situation; a natural refuge may improve
the party's circumstances without converting sleep into a decision screen.
An uninterrupted ordinary Stopover removes up to one Exposure step from each
Citizen, while an interrupted Field Stopover grants no Exposure relief. The
interruption does not cancel the Stopover's elapsed Band, cadence reset, Tharn
ending, or Wound stabilization. A usable Outpost or Rest-Stop Metropolis
removes up to two Exposure steps instead. No Stopover removes the final Away
step; Ancestral Home sleep is uninterrupted but receives only the ordinary
one-step relief.

Occasional situated interruptions keep travel responsive to the country between
Nodes. One 10% check follows an otherwise eligible exhausted travel span, at
most once per Band. An interruption suppresses the next two eligible checks,
and no check occurs in a Band already occupied by RISK or another consequential
MEET. Actual terrain, season, time, weather, and world state choose the content.
A minor interruption changes or reveals the Field directly within TRAVEL; only
a consequential choice opens MEET. Nodes remain the primary intentional Away
content. Appendix G provides the exact TRAVEL, Stopover, and interruption
procedures.

## 5.3 RISK: Crossing

**RISK is the Away Register for crossing a Roadway.** From a **Staging Post** at
the road edge, the party reads traffic, weather, River Spume, Cargo, sightlines,
and far-side refuge, then commits to one complete bodily passage. Its schematic
view is backplated by the actual location and depicts the player party on the
left, the Roadway's moving opposition between, and the destination on the
right. The presentation is deliberately legible rather than realistic.

The player may plan and Commit, wait one Day Band, or resume TRAVEL away from
the road. Commit is the final strategic input:

> **Plan → Commit → Continuous Run → Resolve → Continue**

| Core Species | RISK asks | Planned passage |
|---|---|---|
| **Rabbit** | When does the Roadway open? | Choose a traffic window, broad line, and far-side refuge; sprint once. |
| **Mouse** | Where can continuity be made? | Join three to six body-credible pavement features; scurry through the chain. |
| **Squirrel** | How can movement flow through traffic? | Set one trajectory, one or two redirects, and a far-side anchor; complete the vector run. |

The Colony's Core Species governs the whole party's RISK grammar. Land-bound
Guests act through their actual bodies within it. Crow and Gull alone use a
parallel **Flyer** expression during the same commitment, with River Spume,
weather, visibility, launch, and landing governing a separately visible result.
Flight bypasses neither time, Exposure, nor consequence.

The Roadway never pauses for steering, reaction prompts, or a mid-road MEET.
Every committed Crossing brings the whole party fully to the far side; group
passage resolves first, then delay, Exposure, separation, Cargo loss, Wound,
Maiming, Tharn, death, or another valid consequence applies. Adversity follows
Body Units rather than Mouse headcount. RISK then gives way to a brief far-side
TRAVEL tableau: the party and applied consequences appear from the reverse of
the Staging Post angle before direct movement resumes from the actual arrival
ground. The tableau is not a MEET. A later situated decision opens MEET only
when it independently crosses the ordinary threshold.

Road Work may close a Staging Post or alter the ordinary RISK conditions across
its multi-Reach footprint. Work that changes after Commit still lets the Run
finish before applying its altered condition or penalty. Appendix F provides
the Road Work reference; Appendix G provides full Crossing and Return Crossing
procedure.

A homeward Staging Post ordinarily uses **Automatic Return**: current
conditions produce a clean passage or bounded penalty after the party reaches
the homeward side and the far-side TRAVEL tableau appears. Conditions able to
support Maiming, Tharn, death, or the complete consequence range invoke **Full
RISK** instead.

## 5.4 Nodes in the Field

A **Node** is a fixed, recognizable place whose material, ecology, shelter,
inhabitants, alteration, or history can matter across visits. Terrain,
infrastructure, human activity, season, and prior events establish what exists
there; approach, visibility, opposition, renewal, and present state determine
what the party can do. **Hazard** is a current condition or present animal at a
Node, while memory attaches to the place's history rather than forming another
Node type.

TRAVEL reveals and reaches Nodes. A quiet Node may remain observational, brief
uncontested use may resolve within the current Band, and time-consuming work
advances the next Band. Meaningful opposition, danger, opportunity, or change
opens MEET. Resolution updates the Node, party, Cargo, relationship, or world
directly. Material abandoned on arbitrary ground leaves play unless an authored
Node change makes it persistent. Appendices F and G provide the complete Node
reference.

## 5.5 MEET: Consequential Situations

MEET presents bounded choices for a concrete situation. Unopposed situations
show their contextual actions directly. A contested Away MEET always displays
the same five response families in order—active or visibly greyed—so the player
can read both possibility and constraint:

| Response | Direction |
|---|---|
| **CONTEST** | Overcome, resist, hold, seize, protect, or endure directly. |
| **EVADE** | Bypass, escape, hide, distract, or use terrain. |
| **PARLEY** | Ask, offer, trade, bargain, persuade, deceive, or invoke relationship. |
| **YIELD** | Concede one stake, claim, possession, priority, or cost to preserve another. |
| **WITHDRAW** | End participation and leave the objective or situation. |

Each active family contains one response specific to the current Round. Tools,
Supplies, Keepsakes, relationships, and Guest Signatures may open or change an
eligible response; the provisional choice shows exact expressions before
Commit.

The party's shared result resolves before personal consequence. The system
then identifies Citizens causally exposed by what they used, carried,
protected, confronted, or were already suffering. A clear bearer receives the
consequence; equally exposed candidates resolve randomly. Cause, bearer, and
continuing effect appear immediately. Focus may make the same Citizen visible
but never causes or redirects harm. Chapter 1.5 and Appendix G provide the full
multi-Round, choice, expression, Focus, and aftermath procedure.

## 5.6 Exposure and Bodily Consequence

**Exposure** is a hidden per-Citizen measure from 0 through 6. A Citizen's first
RISK of an expedition sets a floor of 1 after the Crossing resolves. A
circumstance that explicitly creates Exposure normally adds one step; an
unstabilized Wound doubles that gain to two unless the same incident created
the Wound. Exposure weights genuine uncertainty toward the serious end of the
consequence range already supported by the situation:

**Exposure escalation chance = 10% × current Exposure**

One hidden check may advance the selected Citizen's result one position within
that valid range. Exposure neither chooses the causal target nor creates new
consequences, and at 6 it supplies no standing penalty to movement, Carry,
choice, Civic Share, control, or aptitude. Body, behavior, voice, narration,
and context convey the accumulating state without naming its level.

RISK first resolves Crossing consequence without pre-existing Exposure or
Wound weighting. Any Exposure awarded by that result then enters ordinary
persistence, including doubling when an earlier unstabilized Wound applies.

| Consequence | Continuing effect |
|---|---|
| **Wound** | One binary, named recoverable injury; it limits only affected actions and doubles new Exposure while unstabilized. |
| **Maiming** | A lasting bodily change accompanied by one acute Wound; the Wound may heal while the Maiming remains. |
| **Tharn** | Acute shutdown during immediate danger; securing the Citizen and completing Stopover replace the former objective. |
| **Death** | A rare final outcome available only when severe fatal stakes were legible before Commit. |

A later Wound changes the existing injury description and retains the greater
of its current remaining recovery and the new authored requirement; Citizens
do not accumulate multiple Wounds. Every authored requirement remains within
one to three Recovery Days. Remedy may stabilize a Wound through
Homecoming, lessen an immediate effect, contain the Wound accompanying Maiming,
or express care through PARLEY. Stopover stabilization lasts through the next three
traveled Bands and their situations.

Tharn occurs only when an immediate event can credibly produce shutdown.
Exposure and an unstabilized Wound increase susceptibility without causing it
alone. Outside RISK, the hidden escalation chance is:

**Tharn escalation chance = min(80%, 10% × (Exposure + 2 if Wounded and
unstabilized))**

The game never previews or labels the possibility. When Tharn strikes during
MEET, the triggering Round resolves, the current MEET remains open, and its
next Round is reskinned around securing that Citizen and withdrawing or
yielding. Another response remains only when it directly enables rescue. When
RISK produces Tharn, the far-side TRAVEL tableau names it after complete
passage; no rescue MEET opens. In either case the party keeps the Citizen and
cannot activate another Node, enter RISK, or resume the former objective until
one completed Stopover ends Tharn. Any Exposure relief follows the Stopover's
location and whether sleep was interrupted. Remedy has no effect upon Tharn.

An uninterrupted ordinary Stopover removes up to one Exposure step; a usable
Outpost or Rest-Stop Metropolis removes up to two. No Stopover removes the final
step Away. At Home, each DAWN removes up to three steps, so maximum Exposure
falls from 6 to 3 and then 0 over two Dawns. Exposure is not presented as a
domestic pressure or recovery meter: after Homecoming it recedes through hidden
DAWN accounting into the calm of DWELL. Wound, Maiming, and Exposure recover
through their separate procedures. Appendices D and G contain the full bodily
consequence, Home recovery, and Tharn reference.

## 5.7 Outposts and Stopovers

A **Stopover** is the committed Away action and locked screen through which the
party sleeps at its current location. Its static DWELL-like view shows the
party and actual ground while Day-accounting overlays resolve the elapsed
Band and known effects. An **Outpost** is persistent, uninhabited Away
infrastructure that makes one eligible known Stopover reliable.

The Colony establishes or restores an Outpost through the ordinary Home
Project Queue. One usable Workshop supplies capacity while named Builders,
material, and Civic Share-Days remain committed. Routine construction travel
and labor are abstracted into that civic commitment, producing no Away party,
Exposure, or personal consequence. Completion at DAWN establishes the remote
structure and releases the Workshop and Builders.

Explicit visible events alone damage or destroy an Outpost. Damage leaves a
persistent physical shelter but removes protected Stopover, Exposure reduction,
Cargo transfer, and Supply selection until restoration. Outposts have no
stationed Citizens, routine upkeep, specialization, upgrade, or Tier in v0.5.

A Stopover at a usable Outpost is uninterrupted. It advances one Band, resets
travel cadence, ends Tharn, stabilizes Wounds for three traveled Bands, removes
up to two Exposure steps without removing the final Away step, and protects
ordinary sleep from exposed-ground consequences. The player may drain any fungible
Cargo from Carry into Stores through abstract Colony sync, immediately freeing
that Carry. The same sync lets any Citizen with an empty Supply position choose
one available prepared Supply of any class from Colony Stores. The selected
unit leaves Stores and fills that one position; an occupied Supply cannot be
exchanged, and no additional position or other Item is created. These
logistical selections do not make sleeping a decision or turn Stopover into
MEET. The party remains Away, and the Outpost never becomes another Home.
Appendix G provides the full Outpost and Stopover reference.

## 5.8 Return and Homecoming

The player turns homeward through ordinary TRAVEL. Terrain, time, burden,
bodily state, Nodes, Stopover, MEET, and Return Crossings continue through the same
physical world. Clearing the final Roadway returns the party to the Home Median;
Homecoming begins only when it reaches the Colony itself.

**Homecoming** is the state-transition MEET that ends every expedition and
receives its changed Citizens into shared Home life. It advances one Day Band
and follows:

> **Frame → Recognize and receive → Reconcile → Continue**

Recognition shows who returned, anyone absent, visible personal change, the
material result, and the Colony that now receives them before presenting
totals. Reconciliation then transfers Cargo, returns unspent Supplies, clears
their expedition Supply positions, preserves every resolved Item, bodily,
relational, and remembered fact, restores eligible Role contribution, and
recalculates Civic Balance and Home systems.

Most facts reconcile automatically. Care, Allocation, Guest, succession,
memorial, or another choice opens only when its alternatives create materially
different persistent results. A returning Wounded Citizen adds Patient Load
and occupies an available Hearth automatically when only one valid allocation
exists. Competing Patients or occupied Hearths open a Care choice; lack of a
Hearth postpones Recovery Days but never prevents Homecoming itself. Exposure
begins its Home recovery rather than disappearing at the boundary.

A routine Homecoming is brief and warm. A consequential return expands around
its actual concerns without rerolling, grading, or retroactively improving what
happened Away. Completion ends the expedition and restores DWELL. Quiet
Equilibrium and EMBODY return only when the Colony's resulting state supports
them. Appendix G provides the complete recognition, care, allocation,
reconciliation, and reintegration reference.


## 5.9 Landscape Voice: The Playable Page

Landscape Voice is an immediate presentation ambition: playing MEDIAN should
sometimes feel like reading a page in a book whose events the player is causing.
It accompanies and interprets present play as it occurs.

Landscape Voice is MEDIAN's human third-person narrator. Its intended literary
feel draws from the landscape-attentive narrators of Richard Adams and J. R. R.
Tolkien: a voice able to describe country, weather, movement, and simple events
with clarity and weight. Adams and Tolkien establish critical lineage for an
original MEDIAN voice. Landscape Voice stands outside the animal community; it
is distinct from animal speech and from the animal cultural voice that may
shape Chronicle, Tale, or scene presentation.

### The playable page

- Visual composition and prose working together like an illustrated page
- The player continuing to act, read the world, and cause events rather than
  watching a literary cutscene
- The narrator able to describe named Citizens as actors without making any
  Citizen the story's protagonist or point of view
- No first-person Citizen narration, selected viewpoint character, rotating
  party narrator, or access to private interior thought
- Landscape remaining present during action because terrain determines
  perception, movement, safety, and choice
- Narration able to widen into observation or contract around danger
- Sentence length, rhythm, and perceptual breadth reflecting the actual tempo
  of play
- Landscape Voice using established history for continuity when relevant
  without belonging to Campaign Memory or requiring a Record transaction

### What the Voice attends to

- Locating the party plainly within Day Band, light, weather, direction, and
  the ground immediately around it
- Following collective movement through successive terrain: leaving one kind
  of cover, entering another, climbing, descending, spreading out, catching
  up, keeping together, or following a physical feature
- Allowing an uneventful stretch of TRAVEL to pass in a few concrete sentences
  without pretending that nothing existed between mechanical destinations
- Moving attention naturally between the animals, their near ground, middle
  distance, horizon, and sky
- Naming vegetation, water, soil, weather, infrastructure, and distance when
  those facts make the place more exact
- Letting small external events—a change of wind, diminishing light, moving
  water, a distant animal call, or the return of silence—complete a passage
- Giving ordinary actions weight through their location and timing rather than
  manufacturing plot, suspense, or psychological revelation
- Using natural description during quiet travel as readily as during danger or
  discovery
- Favoring concrete sequence and restrained observation over ornamental lore,
  constant metaphor, or summary of what the player should feel

### One narrator, several bodies

- Mouse bodies making seams, edges, cavities, enclosure, and fine passages
  materially relevant to the events the narrator describes
- Rabbit bodies making cover, exposed distance, shared ground, and warning
  materially relevant
- Squirrel bodies making anchors, height, gaps, momentum, and continuity of
  passage materially relevant
- Guest bodies likewise changing which physical details matter when their
  actual capabilities or vulnerabilities enter the event
- Mixed parties remaining together in one narrated landscape rather than
  dividing the passage into competing viewpoints
- The same human narrator describing these bodily relationships without
  adopting an animal's cultural voice or creating separate realities,
  cognitive limits, or rigid species prose templates

### Truth and interpretation

- Landscape Voice remaining authoritative about situated perception and
  restrained about interpretation
- Current terrain, weather, light, sound, visibility, party composition,
  bodily condition, established relationships, and resolved consequences able
  to inform a passage
- No hidden knowledge, undiscovered hazard, unresolved outcome, unsupported
  emotion, or moral conclusion entering through narration
- No essential mechanical instruction being conveyed only through prose
- The voice changing tempo and attention without changing truth

### Earned use

- Candidate moments including entry into a new or changed landscape, first
  sight of a significant destination, emergence from constricted terrain, an
  overlook, the far Margin after a consequential Crossing, the resolved
  aftermath of a spatially important MEET, return through familiar ground, and
  Homecoming after meaningful absence
- Landscape Voice remaining scarce enough for its appearance to retain weight
- Prose entering unobtrusively and yielding naturally to ordinary input rather
  than imposing a cutscene or fixed wait
- The game remaining mechanically complete when a passage is absent, with the
  passage succeeding when it deepens the player's apprehension of place,
  action, and participating lives
- No sixth Register, reward, progression system, memory category, narrative
  currency, or parallel authority

### Landscape as syntax

**Terrain → perception → available choices → movement → consequence**

- Physical landscape producing action and meaning rather than serving as
  interchangeable decoration
- Human infrastructure becoming geography, ecology, refuge, boundary, danger,
  and territory at animal scale

# PART VI — THE CAMPAIGN

## 6.1 Campaign Memory

**Campaign Memory** is the one historical architecture through which the game
retains what happened and presents what the Colony remembers.

```text
Record — hidden factual history
├── Chronicle — selected Colony history
├── Citizen Tales — history applying to particular Citizens
├── Item Tales — history applying to particular Items
└── canon images — the evolving visual record of Citizens and the Colony
```

The **Record** is an incremental, machine-readable account of resolved facts.
Home and Away write to the same authority; later written or visual
presentations may select, consolidate, and translate those facts but never
replace them. The player ordinarily encounters the **Chronicle**, subject-bound
**Tales**, and evolving canon imagery rather than the underlying database.

The Chronicle expresses shared civic and cultural history. A Citizen or
meaning-bearing Item has a Tale shaped around the facts that apply to that
subject; Founders and arriving Citizens may also bring a **Prior-life Tale**
whose established facts enter the Record as known prior history. Supplies remain
anonymous units and receive no Tale.

Every Citizen has one current canon image, while the Colony uses the smallest
current representation set capable of preserving its spatial, material, and
inhabited identity. Lasting visible change may amend those images; dramatic
scenes, Register transitions, information panels, Chronicle and Tale
illustrations, Homecoming, and milestones reuse them as mandatory references
without creating new facts.

The **Almanac** stands outside Campaign Memory. It presents current state and
known, scheduled, forecast, or possible future events; resolution removes a
prospect from that view, updates the present, and writes what actually happened
to the Record. Given Names, Distinctions, rare After-names, death, departure,
destruction, and memorial continuity all follow this same factual history.
Appendix C provides the complete Record-to-Presentation and canon-image
reference; Appendix B provides naming and narrative-language rules.

## 6.2 The opening campaign

The opening campaign mechanically enacts the Founding Escape narrated in Part
0. It lets the player briefly inhabit a mature ancestral Home, then chains
MEDIAN's ordinary Registers into a directed **cut sequence** whose looking,
movement, selection, and first Crossing remain playable.

Part 0's named Rabbit account is the Sourcebook's illustration of this
structure. Actual play generates the selected Species' ancestral Colony,
Founders, names, Places, and remembered particulars before presenting the same
mechanical sequence; none of the Story-Civilization subjects is a prewritten
campaign character or location.

### Mechanical sequence

1. **Reach 4 flash-forward.** Open wordlessly on the exhausted Founders running
   toward the final smaller Reach-border road, then return to calm before
   granting control.
2. **Ancestral Colony — guided DWELL.** Let the player inspect a grand inhabited
   expression of the chosen Core Species while reserving lasting management for
   the Colony they will found.
3. **A lesson — short EMBODY.** A future Founder observes a Leader conducting
   a lesson for young Citizens at the Gathering Place.
4. **Chaos — state-transition MEET.** Animal-scale vibration, sound, dust,
   obscured sight, and failing ground destroy the old Home and bring the
   escaping Founders into focus.
5. **First flight — guided TRAVEL.** Three Rabbits or Squirrels—or four Mice—run
   upcorridor through playable Field movement.
6. **First Reach border — playable RISK.** A lightly trafficked smaller road
   teaches the chosen species' ordinary Crossing grammar and carries the party
   into Reach 2.
7. **Long escape — authored montage.** Continued TRAVEL and two Crossings
   provide brief apprehensions of Reaches 3 and 4 before returning to the
   opening composition.
8. **Final border — montage Crossing.** The party crosses into Reach 5 with
   every Founder at Exposure 6.
9. **Promising ground — Landscape Voice.** Quiet, air, ground, vegetation, and
   bodily relief resolve into the recognition that it feels good here.
10. **Founding — state-transition MEET.** The player inspects the ground,
    commits **Make Home**, establishes the first Leader, assembles the Colony's
    name, and enters ordinary DWELL before the first construction commitment.

The sequence preserves each Register's ordinary visual and mechanical
identity while constraining its immediate subject and direction. The first
EMBODY opens from Quiet Equilibrium in the ancestral Home, establishing
ordinary intimacy before interruption. The first Crossing excludes fatality,
Maiming, and Tharn from its authored consequence range. Destruction, flight,
and four real Crossings bring every Founder to
maximum hidden Exposure without automatically creating Wound or Tharn. The
Reach 4 flash-forward gains meaning when the montage returns to it rather than
through an explanatory chronology label.

### Founding the permanent Home

Reach 5 realizes one of the selected Core Species' two founding-biome
relationships. Landscape Voice enters only after the final Crossing, when the
country becomes perceptible again. The founding MEET treats **Make Home** as an
explicit state transition.

The Founder who received Focus during the decisive Chaos Round appears as the
default first Leader, though the player may select another. One Founder must
sustain Leader before speaking for the group in the player's sole direct Colony
naming decision. The ancestral Colony's name remains visible while the player
assembles the new one from curated, species-influenced lists of words, sounds,
and forms through approved patterns. Ground, hope, and memory shape suggestions
without restricting their possible meaning. Confirmation makes the complete
name permanent and records it as part of Founding.

### Early Colony development

Rabbit and Squirrel begin with three Founders; Mouse begins with four. Every
Founder arrives at Exposure 6, then recovers to 3 at the first DAWN and 0 at
the second. The territory has no completed Residence or Practice. A fixed
reserve supports the initial three-Body-Unit recovery and contains material for
the first Workshop followed by either one Residence or one Garden; the player
may choose which unmet need remains consequential.

Those carried resources begin as pooled Stores in a visible provisional cache
on the founding ground. Selecting the cache or using the global menu opens the
same Resources screen. The cache supplies no Practice Strength or Caretaker
Provisioning. After the first Workshop exists, the Colony may establish an
ordinary Storehouse Project; until then, the cache preserves resource access
without becoming another bootstrap exception.

Immediately after **Make Home**, the Mouse opening introduces two named
Wanderers through an authored Home MEET. They join as complete Citizens with
their own relationships and Tales, bringing six Mice to the same three-Body-Unit
civic scale as the other openings before ordinary construction begins. This
specific welcome grants no automatic rule for later Wanderers.

DWELL introduces Civic Balance by showing three Body Units of Housing Pressure.
The tutorial guides one Rabbit or Squirrel—or suggests two Mice—into Builder,
then establishes the first Workshop through its sole bootstrap exemption. Place
designation costs nothing; the Practice requires the Project. One Mouse remains
a legal Builder but contributes one-half Civic Share-Day, so the same Project
takes twice as long as with two Mice or one full-share Builder.

Workshop completion opens ordinary Project capacity and the first strategic
choice: a Residence answers Housing Pressure, while a Garden enables passive
Sustenance. A Well-Placed first Residence houses all three opening Body Units;
a Not-Well-Placed one leaves one Body Unit uncovered. The unchosen need remains
visible until answered.

### The first expeditions

The Home Median may contain Sustenance and minor Scrap Nodes. The first Node
encountered there may be uncontested Sustenance; the first Node encountered in
a Margin is Contested. At least one Guest species appears within the first four
post-founding MEETs, introducing the wider animal community without requiring
immediate adoption.

The first Launch uses the ordinary legal party, visible Items, departure facts,
and Home subtraction preview without revealing an unseen Node or prescribing a
solution. The player chooses whether to explore the Home Median or cross toward
a Margin, when to turn back, and what returns. The first full Homecoming
recognizes the named travelers, reconciles all material and personal facts,
advances its ordinary Day Band, restores eligible Civic Shares, writes the
established history, and releases the campaign into open play.

## 6.3 Civic progression

Colony Tiers recognize a stable scale already reached and give it a shared
historical name. Tier number is common mechanical vocabulary; each Core Species
expresses that scale through its own civic form.

| Tier | Required living Body Units | Mouse | Rabbit | Squirrel |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **I** | Founding | **First Rooms** | **Close Commons** | **First Anchors** |
| **II** | 7 | **Joined House** | **Open Commons** | **Linked Ways** |
| **III** | 12 | **Manor House** | **Court** | **Living Web** |
| **IV** | 18 | **Grand Manor** | **Grand Court** | **Grand Web** |

Population is the sole eligibility gate. Every living named Citizen contributes
their Body Unit regardless of age, health, availability, or current location.
Tier II recognizes a Colony able to prepare beyond its next immediate need;
Tier III recognizes a mature, durable Home able to endure and choose; Tier IV
is optional grand ambition.

Recognition additionally requires Quiet Equilibrium, an available Home-present
Citizen sustaining Leader, and a large escalating **Civic Dedication** outside
protected reserves. Committing it consumes its resources and one Day Band and
opens the Recognition MEET. The material becomes species-specific built fabric
rather than a Place, Practice, inventory object, numerical Capacity, or Project
Queue entry. Exact recipes belong to the dedicated economy-tuning phase.

The player may defer an eligible recognition indefinitely. The active Leader
conducts the ceremony, with universal Focus rules resolving any equally
eligible clash. Recognition proceeds one Tier at a time, enters Campaign
Memory, and remains historical truth through later contraction, Pressure, or
damage. Appendix D provides the complete Tier and Recognition reference.

## 6.4 Corridor progression

Corridor progression is the continuous world the Colony has reached,
experienced, and made practically accessible. Reaches and Margins exist from
the beginning; ordinary TRAVEL, RISK, MEET, relationships, and Outpost Projects
accumulate an outward history from the permanent center of Home.

Distance, Exposure, Stopover cadence, Carry, bodily state, physical obstruction,
and the necessary return determine practical range. Natural refuges and
Outposts make longer travel credible while every intervening path and Crossing
remains continuous. Several Outposts may form a useful geographic chain without
becoming a network score or separate progression system.

Progress appears as revisitable change: a relationship alters a formerly
Contested Node, a resource site recovers or depletes, shelter becomes reliable,
a Guest or settlement recognizes the party, or a distant place enters ordinary
range. The map and Almanac present those established facts directly.

Social access remains particular to the involved animals, venues, and
relationships rather than becoming universal reputation. Colony scale and
Corridor ambition remain independent: a small Colony may travel far when it can
bear the domestic subtraction and risk, while a large Colony may remain close
to Home. Corridor development continues after any Tier plateau. Civic
Dedication gives recovered material a major Home use without making exploration
compulsory. Appendices F and G provide the exact world, Outpost, and expedition
reference.

## 6.5 Landmark Reaches in the Campaign

Part II establishes Landmark Reaches as exceptional whole-Reach identities
within the continuous Corridor. In campaign play they are reached through
ordinary expeditions whenever practical range permits. Their Nodes and MEETs
use the normal Away Registers and persistent world state. Three forms give the
Corridor its broadest long-horizon contrasts:

- **The Ancestral Home:** one generated and heavily authored lost society
- **Interchange Expanses:** recurring Corridor abundance and plurality
  organized by crisscrossing Roadways and immediate circumstance
- **The Rest-Stop Metropolis:** plurality gathered into a lasting city

### The Ancestral Home

Every campaign generates its own ancestral Colony for the Founding Escape:
its name, Founders, Places, civic arrangement, material culture, and remembered
particulars belong to that campaign. The selected Core Species shapes this
generation without selecting a fixed Colony. Elderbank, Nine Beeches, Chaff
End, and all of their named Citizens and Places are Sourcebook illustrations
only; none is fixed game content.

The Ancestral Home generates once at the location established by the Founding
Escape, some ways down the Corridor from permanent Home. It fills its Median
Reach rather than forming one ruin icon inside generic terrain. Its physical
TRAVEL layout uses the Interchange Expanse's broad multi-Roadway grammar:
several roads may divide or cross the Reach, some at Crossing ground level and
others overhead or otherwise illustrative.

Its Node slate is deliberately different. Specialist remains derive from the
actual opening Colony, while authored story-Node functions bind to compatible
campaign Places, paths, objects, Citizens, relationships, and memories.
Present Resources, shelter, inhabitants, and hazards remain possible, but the
Reach does not use an ordinary random Node and encounter mix or inherit the
Interchange Expanse's emphasis on renewable abundance.

The old Colony is neither a ruin dungeon nor a restoration opportunity. Its
campaign value lies in exploration, recovery, recognition, and reckoning while
the present territory remains materially alive. A returning Founder or
authored Wanderer escapee may open a Remembrance MEET through lived connection
to an exact place, loss, relationship, possession, or memory. A low-frequency
encounter may reveal one lone escapee. Neither possibility is advertised in
advance. The result may enter Campaign Memory; the new Colony remains the sole
Home.

An Outpost may occupy one suitable sheltered site, extending practical range
without converting the old Colony into an annex or replacement Home.

### Interchange Expanses

Interchange Expanses can autogenerate elsewhere in the Corridor as Landmark
Reaches. Unlike the ordinary Interchange biome, each Expanse changes the
playable Reach's cross-section itself.

Ramps, barriers, underpasses, pillars, drains, embankments, divided grades, and
adjoining Roadways create several connected grounds. Some Roadways require
Crossing at ground level. Elevated Roadways remain decorative infrastructure
expressed through Staging Post vistas, TRAVEL composition, and Node MEET
backplates. A below-grade Roadway may form an impassable interior boundary.

Formal Margins disappear at the longitudinal Crossing into the Expanse.
The whole non-road Reach functions as Roadway-influenced Field, with actual
distance from the nearest ground-level Roadway preserving River Spume and
proximity effects. Ordinary Median and Margin geography resumes in the next
Reach without requiring an in-world explanation for the transition.

The shortest legible passage through the Reach requires one ground-level
Crossing. Richer optional grounds may demand additional movement or Crossings;
a generator may contrast one direct difficult Crossing with a longer path
through two narrower roads. This remains direct-drive TRAVEL and ordinary RISK,
not a route-planning layer.

Interchange Expanses are lush with persistent Nodes. Programmatic **Perishable
Sustenance**, **Durable Sustenance**, **Rigid Scrap**, and **Flexible Scrap**
Nodes all occur more frequently than in ordinary Reaches, while a separately
determined higher percentage of those Resource Nodes are Contested. Exact
density and contest percentages remain tuning.

Each Node retains a concrete world-facing material identity suited to its
ground, such as berry bushes, seed concentrations, discarded food, wire,
cloth, plastic, wood, or metal. Recovery assigns its yield to the applicable
one or more of the four existing fungible categories. By entry into the
Colony's pooled Stores, equivalent units no longer retain separate source
identities. Interchange Expanse generation creates no additional resource
class or currency.

A Resource Node may be single-category, such as a berry bush, or
variable-category, such as a trash pile. Variable Nodes commonly yield one to
three categories and do not divide their yield evenly: one category is
primary, with smaller secondary or tertiary yields where present. Exact
quantities and proportions remain tuning.

Twelve authored Interchange Expanse Story MEET forms provide a separate content layer:
**Divided Plenty**, **The Throwing Place**, **The Fallen Load**, **The Unusual
Object**, **Occupied Refuge**, **Camp in Transition**, **The Spanning Claim**,
**Underpass Meeting Ground**, **Converging Travelers**, **The Isolated
Animal**, **Changed Drainage**, and **The Brief Truce**. Together they cover
material abundance, habitation and territorial use, animal convergence, and
environmental change without making those editorial distinctions into
player-facing categories.

Each generated Interchange Expanse selects three to six compatible forms without
replacement and installs each selected form once as a particular persistent
situation fitted to the actual geography, Nodes, and inhabitants. Every
selected set includes at least one animal-centered form and at least one
infrastructure- or ecology-centered form. Selection does not guarantee
immediate discovery; a form may remain dormant until eligible. It never
rerolls as a fresh copy on later visits, although its participants,
relationships, Node, and aftermath may continue to develop through ordinary
rules. No form guarantees a Guest, reward, safe refuge, or favorable
resolution.

Travelers, regulars, scavengers, temporary camps, territorial animals, and
predators meet in unusual variety. Claims, exchanges, warnings, favors,
grudges, shelters, and agreements remain local and particular. Story MEET
content is independent of the programmatic Resource-Node contest percentage.

Familiar relationships change later encounters without creating universal
safety, a governing society, or a map-wide reputation. An Outpost may occupy
one suitable sheltered site while leaving the wider Reach under its many local
arrangements.

### The Rest-Stop Metropolis

The Metropolis is a large persistent Field territory experienced through
TRAVEL. Every Node is unique to that particular civic territory. Markets,
shelters, workshops, gathering places, gardens, refuse sites, and other venues
form a living network of authored specialist Nodes rather than drawing from
the ordinary programmatic Resource-Node generator. Sustenance, Scrap, trade,
shelter, and other resource opportunities belong to those particular places.

Rest-Stop Metropolis has no programmatic Contested percentage. This does not
prohibit authored opposition, bargaining, incompatible claims, or other
consequential city MEETs. Arrival, important transactions, hospitality,
consequential encounters, and departure use MEET; its uninterrupted substantial
Stopover removes up to two Exposure steps from each Citizen without removing
the final Away step.

Dependable venues support trade, shelter, specialized craft, information,
Guest encounters, and Practice-improvement understanding. Relationships change
how particular residents and venues receive the party. Metropolis may visually
echo DWELL, but Home is shaped by the player while the city is learned through
navigation and participation.

Four authored venue forms establish the present service foundation:

| Authored Node | Service and boundary |
|---|---|
| **Tool repair and trade venue** | Repairs a damaged Tool or makes an ordinary new Tool from ingredients supplied in party Cargo. A Citizen needs an empty Tool position to receive a new Tool and may discard a held Tool to free it; the discarded Tool leaves play unless the authored transaction explicitly accepts it. The party supplies the ordinary Tool recipe plus one additional Resource unit of a type already used by that recipe, chosen by the venue. Because ordinary Tool repair is covered work at Home, Metropolis repair instead costs one materially relevant Resource unit chosen by the venue. |
| **Resource shopkeeper venue** | Offers a particular visible stock of Resources for barter against the party's actual Cargo. Stock, accepted exchange, resident, relationship, and later availability belong to this persistent Node. No currency, universal price list, or detached shop inventory is created. |
| **Food-preservation venue** | Converts **3 Perishable Sustenance into 2 Durable Sustenance** within party Cargo. This is the ordinary one-for-one Colony transformation for two output units plus one additional Perishable unit as the Metropolis service premium. |
| **Scrap-transformer venue** | Converts either **3 Flexible Scrap into 2 Rigid Scrap** or **3 Rigid Scrap into 2 Flexible Scrap** within party Cargo. No equivalent transformation exists at Home; the three-for-two loss is the unique service's complete authored cost rather than a Colony recipe plus premium. |

The general rule is: **a service duplicated at Home and Rest-Stop Metropolis
uses its ordinary Colony recipe plus one additional Resource unit.** The
additional unit must be a Resource type already used by the underlying recipe
and is chosen by the authored venue. When the Home service is ordinarily
covered work with no material recipe, as with ordinary Tool repair, the venue
chooses one materially credible Resource as its cost. A service unique to the
Metropolis has authored terms of its own. Routine visible transactions resolve
at their Nodes; bargaining, relationship, incompatible terms, or another
consequential choice may open MEET. All inputs and outputs belong to party
Cargo or the involved Citizen's Item position, never remote Colony Stores.

All three forms and the Corridor beyond remain available as continuing
sources of exploration, relationship, and change. MEDIAN v0.5 has no formal
victory condition.


# PART VII — PRESENTATION AND DEVELOPMENT BOUNDARY

## 7.1 Interface and information design

MEDIAN presents one persistent world through five Registers. Each changes what
the player attends to and how they act while preserving the same Citizens,
Places, objects, time, and consequences. The world remains visually primary;
the interface makes known facts, meaningful uncertainty, available actions,
and the consequences of commitment legible within it.

### Shared interface grammar

- Inspection, navigation, and provisional selection pause time.
- A discrete commitment identifies its immediate cost and affected subjects
  before it advances time or exposes them to consequence. Direct TRAVEL
  movement instead makes its accumulating distance and next Day-Band boundary
  continuously legible.
- Information appears beside the Citizen, Place, Node, Item, or situation it
  concerns where practical; broader detail remains available through
  inspection.
- Resolution stays in the present scene, and changed facts show what happened
  and why without detached reward screens or success grades.
- Register transitions preserve visual, spatial, and historical continuity.

Selection is reversible inspection; **Commit** changes the world. The player
may revise a provisional selection while time is paused. Commit uses one clear
grammar across Registers and avoids a second confirmation when the action and
its consequence are already unmistakable. Multiple-Round MEETs retain the
scene while updating its facts, stakes, and responses.

### Visible and private state

Actionable Colony facts—including Civic Shares, Role Load, Readiness, Role
Balance, Housing Balance, and known Telegraphs—remain legible. Colony Pressure
and Exposure remain private calculations, expressed through known causes,
Telegraphs, rest need, Wound, Tharn, and other experienced consequences.
Fractional production and Project contributions accumulate unseen until whole
results are awarded at DAWN.

The Record remains a hidden factual back end. The Almanac, Chronicle, Tales,
Citizen views, maps, and canon images present appropriate selections from
established state. The Community Board mirrors selected Almanac information
inside DWELL without gating access to it.

Interfaces show possibilities relevant to the actual task. Launch presents
only mechanically eligible Citizens; Core Citizens never present refusal or
personality approval because they are fractional player characters. Ordinary
MEETs show their authored responses. A relevant unavailable response remains
visible only when its absence matters, with a concise explanation based on
known facts; irrelevant responses are absent. Contested Away MEETs always show
**CONTEST → EVADE → PARLEY → YIELD → WITHDRAW** in that order, greying an
inactive family in place. Provisional response selection exposes only the
Tools, Supplies, Keepsakes, relationships, and Signatures that can affect it.
Focus resolves after Commit, randomly among equally qualified Citizens.

### Presentation by Register

#### DWELL

DWELL is an elevated, isometric-style operational view of the Colony, with
free rotation, spatial sliding, and continuous zoom. Every Core Colony and
every operationally relevant part of its life remain above ground and visible:
Citizens, rooms, Places, Practices, Projects, paths, Stores, common space, and
Civic Balance can be understood as one inhabited arrangement. The camera may
clarify overlaps while preserving the implied physical Place.

Selection, Place designation, construction, Project commitment, and Role
assignment occur directly through the Colony. Close views reveal particular
lives without leaving collective stewardship. Mouse preserves the feeling of
interior life through dense above-ground rooms, shared walls, joined edges,
covered links, and accumulated shelter rather than a separate underground
layer.

A Storehouse gives pooled Stores a visible Home presence. Selecting any
Storehouse opens the globally available Resources screen; several Storehouses
never create separate inventories. Before the first is established, the same
screen remains available through the founding cache and global interface.

#### TRAVEL

TRAVEL is a top-down, direct-drive overworld view. Directional input moves one
animated party figure—composed from the named Citizens present—through a
continuous Field while terrain, Nodes, shelter, Carry, bodily state, rest need,
and the remaining span of the Day Band stay readable around it. A stable
camera, limited paused panning, and modest zoom preserve both animal scale and
terrain legibility.

Actual path length, terrain, conditions, and burden consume the current travel
span; Rabbit's multiplier extends it. A Node becomes distinct before
engagement opens MEET. A Roadway occupies the approaching edge before its
Staging Post takes focus and RISK begins. Inspection and resolved encounters
return the party to its exact Field position and remaining span.

Committing to sleep opens the **Stopover screen**, not another Register or a
MEET. The camera locks into a static DWELL-like composition of the party and
its exact Field location while informational overlays show Day accounting,
cadence reset, bodily-state effects, and any place-specific protection or
transfer. Completion returns to TRAVEL at that same position. A regular-Field
interruption may open a separate MEET; Stopover at a usable Outpost, the
Ancestral Home, or Rest-Stop Metropolis is uninterrupted.

#### RISK

RISK is a species-shaped Crossing schematic grounded by the party's actual
Staging Post view across the Roadway. The backplate carries place, weather,
traffic, River Spume, danger, and far-side refuge; the normalized schematic
carries planning information and the complete proposed passage.

- Rabbit reads the broad traffic pattern, an opening, the party's complete
  line, and the selected refuge.
- Mouse links three to six pavement-scale, body-credible points into one
  continuous scurry.
- Squirrel reads flowing traffic geometry through one or two redirects and a
  far-side anchor.

Land-bound Guests remain within the Core Species grammar, constrained by their
actual bodies. Crow and Gull flight is a parallel Flyer expression in the same
Crossing. Commit closes planning and animates one uninterrupted Continuous
Run, with no mid-Crossing input. Resolution carries the whole party to the far
side, then returns to TRAVEL through a brief reverse-angle arrival tableau that
applies and displays bounded consequences before direct movement resumes. The
tableau is not a MEET; a later situation opens MEET only through the ordinary
threshold.

#### MEET

MEET is a presentational scene-and-matrix composition backplated by its actual
Node, part of Home, Staging Post, or other owning Place. The player-aligned
subject appears on the left; the answering animal, condition, opportunity, or
threat appears on the right. This **antagonist** is a dramatic position, not a
requirement for hostility or personhood.

A concise subject and one to three stakes frame a stable choice band.
Provisional selection expands a response in place to show applicable objects,
relationships, Signatures, costs, and likely direction. Commit resolves
through the staged figures, sound, animation, speech, and Focus. A later Round
retains place and participants while changed facts replace those resolved.
Launch, Homecoming, Recognition, and other MEET state transitions use the same
grammar with procedures fitted to their subjects. Stopover instead uses its
locked static sleep view. Time pauses for
consideration, though ambient motion may continue without changing state.

#### EMBODY

Ordinary EMBODY enters through one present and available Citizen in a safe,
usable part of Home. The DWELL view closes toward that Citizen and settles into
an intimate frame, ordinarily close third person, that can move between
participating as the Citizen and remaining with them. Operational summaries
recede while the Citizen, immediate Place, weather, movement, sound, touch, and
nearby relationships carry the experience.

Controls are bounded to the activity. The Citizen retains their particular
posture, pace, response, and social behavior; other Citizens remain named
inhabitants rather than controllable scenery. Open Table instead enters through
an eligible gathering and settles into guided collective Presence at animal
scale. The player inhabits no Citizen and may direct attention among a large
cast and several simultaneous activities without returning to DWELL's
operational view. It requires a safe usable Gathering Place carrying the Open
Table improvement and a present authored gathering.
Departure returns to DWELL without penalty. Loss of Quiet Equilibrium ends the
experience safely and lets an urgent situation open MEET from its owning
Register.

### Information lenses and active attention

Paused Registers provide contextual inspection and the principal information
lenses without losing camera, selection, location, or provisional choice:

- **Almanac:** current known state, Telegraphs, schedules, forecasts, and
  meaningful uncertainty
- **Map:** known geography, party position, projected reach, discovered Nodes,
  Outposts, shelter, and world changes
- **Roster and Citizen view:** belonging, location, availability, body, Role,
  relationships, possessions, current canon image, and Tale
- **Chronicle and Tales:** player-facing Campaign Memory selections

Visible persistent subjects open their appropriate contextual view directly.
MEET permits inspection of known participants and capabilities before Commit;
full reference closes during a committed Continuous Run and returns at its
resolution. Each authoritative fact receives one functional presentation
rather than parallel ledgers.

Home and Away are operationally exclusive and informationally permeable.
Launch transfers operation from DWELL to the one active expedition; Homecoming
restores ordinary DWELL. While Away, Home views permit inspection but not
construction, Role assignment, Project commitment, or another Launch. The
Colony continues through its established civic state, while the party never
moves, rests, interacts, or decides autonomously when attention is elsewhere.
DAWN reports completed Colony accounting before returning to the party. A
consequential Home MEET may temporarily take focus and then return operation to
the party's persistent Field position.

### Controls and accessibility

Every action is fully remappable across keyboard, mouse, controller, and
equivalent directional input. Inspection, planning, selection, and decisions
remain untimed. RISK planning can pause or advance observed traffic in readable
steps, while the Continuous Run requires no reaction input. DWELL camera
movement does not require simultaneous precision input; EMBODY uses bounded
controls and permits departure without penalty.

Text scaling, contrast, captions, interface density, camera motion, shake,
flashing, and transition intensity are adjustable. Important information uses
text, shape, position, and icon as well as color. Accessibility changes
presentation and input demands while preserving the same world state and
authored decisions.

## 7.2 Art and sound

### Visual identity

MEDIAN uses grounded stylized realism at animal scale. Fur, feather, grass,
root, bark, mud, and rain meet asphalt, concrete, salt, rust, rubber, plastic,
drainage metal, and reflected road light. Animal civilization grows within
those materials through scavenging, craft, spatial knowledge, and collective
use.

Home is warm, inhabited, and gently storybook; the Field makes the same
animals small within a colder, less accommodating world. Scale, weather,
sound, obscured visibility, and sudden motion give danger weight while the game
retains color, tenderness, humor, and ordinary civic life. Each Register
expresses this contrast through the presentation defined in Section 7.1.
Night preserves the highway's material identity through headlight wash,
brake-light red, reflective signs, wet asphalt, distant windows, and uneven
darkness.

### Strategic transparency and bodies

All Core Colonies remain visibly operable above ground. The camera may fade a
roof, omit a near wall, thin foliage, or clarify an overlap so rooms, paths,
Citizens, and civic relationships can be read. This **strategic transparency**
belongs to presentation: open to the player does not mean exposed within the
world.

Citizens are fully anthropomorphic characters with recognizable animal bodies.
They speak, build, remember, and form communities, while locomotion, scale,
posture, and capability remain species-grounded. They may stand, grasp, carry,
and gesture as their bodies permit; Crossings place grounded animals on four
feet while Flyers use their parallel expression. Ears, head, stance, gait,
stillness, and relation to others lead expression, supported rather than
replaced by the face.

### Material culture and visual continuity

Natural matter and corridor debris become animal-scale material culture:
bark, shell, grass, roots, leaves, cloth, paper, rubber, plastic, wire, caps,
and fragments are selected and remade. Clothing is selective. Wraps, cloaks,
belts, pouches, weather coverings, and adaptive devices can distinguish and
protect without obscuring fur, feathers, markings, posture, or Keepsakes. Role
may affect what is at hand without becoming a uniform. Civic advancement
improves selection, fit, repair, adaptation, and ornament; grandeur comes from
accumulated care rather than weapons, armor, or adventurer loadouts.

Storehouse follows the same civic function without becoming a miniature human
warehouse. Mouse JOIN integrates fitted cupboards, caches, and receiving edges
into adjoining rooms; Rabbit GATHER makes provisions legible around protected
common access; Squirrel CONNECT distributes secure holdings along linked,
reachable anchors. In every form, the Practice visibly receives and tends the
single pooled Stores state. Particular baskets, shelves, wrappings, and caches
may become MEET stakes without implying item-by-item simulation.

Each Citizen's current canon image anchors species, body, markings, Keepsake,
clothing, Maiming, adaptive devices, and other persistent distinctions across
Roster, MEET, EMBODY, aftermath, transition, Chronicle, and Citizen panels.
Lasting change updates the current image; earlier images remain in secondary
history. A small current Colony image set establishes spatial form and life,
while superseded views preserve meaningful stages of growth, damage, repair,
season, and occasion. Generated setups may change composition, light, posture,
and participants, but must use the relevant canon imagery and preserve current
facts. Appendix C defines the complete reference-and-amend method.

### Animal scale, weather, and sound

Human infrastructure retains its full magnitude. Lane paint, barrier seams,
bolts, tires, grates, culverts, vehicles, signs, and machinery anchor scale and
become terrain, shelter, obstruction, resource, or danger. Giants remain
outside the frame; their world is known through traffic, maintenance, Road
Work, litter, runoff, noise, light, and disturbance. Vehicles and machinery
may appear in full. Force is conveyed through magnitude, speed, air, vibration,
sound, obscured sight, aftermath, and animal reaction rather than explicit
injury spectacle.

Persistent geography changes meaning by Register and state. A culvert may be
terrain, shelter, Node, flood obstruction, or Outpost entrance; a wreck may be
orientation, salvage, or a MEET backplate. Light, Day Band, weather, and season
change the readable condition of the same place. Growth and dieback, water,
wear, repair, ground condition, stored matter, and civic adaptation accumulate
as its material history. Mechanically relevant conditions use aligned motion,
shape, texture, sound, text, and icon rather than a decorative filter.

Sound makes the Corridor readable. Traffic has direction, density, rhythm,
mixture, approach, and mass; River Spume makes its displaced air and debris
visible. Home layers species movement, conversation, work, teaching, weather
against shelter, road noise, and Guest vocal presence. Quiet Equilibrium lets
ordinary civic detail emerge. Field sound opens outward into vegetation,
water, insects, animals, drainage, and human activity; RISK brings traffic
close enough to read gaps and vehicle type by ear. Every important audio cue
has an aligned visual form.

Individual sound remains bodily: footfall, breath, fur or feathers against
material, carried objects, calls, and adaptive devices distinguish species and
Citizens. Maiming changes movement sound consistently and respectfully. Tharn
abruptly narrows the wider field around breath, pulse, fixation, and peril.

Landscape Voice is a distinct human narration of country, weather, movement,
and simple events, separate from animal speech, cultural expression, and
mechanical notification. Music is sparse and adaptive, supporting transitions,
Homecoming, ceremony, wonder, or grief before receding so the place itself can
be heard. It follows what has become perceptible, preserving the shock of
concealed danger.

## 7.3 Content-authoring doctrine

The Authorial Grammar governs terminology, capitalization, semantic
typography, and specification prose. This doctrine governs how an established
system becomes a particular place, situation, choice, image, or passage:

**Author a concrete situation in the physical world, identify the existing
system that owns it, and let the player meet it through the appropriate
Register.**

Begin with state already held by the world, Colony, Place, Node, Citizen,
party, object, relationship, or clock. Give it concrete geography, bodies,
material, time, and circumstances; derive choices from credible affordances;
and return the outcome to its established owner. A bounded authored exception
may express a particular species, Guest, Place, object, or event. A new
universal mechanic requires an explicit design decision.

### Constructing a MEET

Chapter 1.5 owns the shared form. A content author:

1. Names the owning system and facts that make the MEET eligible.
2. States its subject in one concrete sentence.
3. Establishes Place or Node, Day Band, conditions, participants, and known
   uncertainty.
4. Selects one to three present stakes.
5. Authors materially different responses and their availability; contested
   Away MEETs retain the five canonical families and order.
6. Attaches each relevant object, relationship, Signature, or civic commitment
   to the exact response it can express and states what it changes.
7. Resolves shared results before assigning bodily, personal, material, and
   relational consequences through causal involvement.
8. Returns changed facts to their owners and identifies any credible
   continuation or Campaign Memory expression.

An ordinary decision offers at least two meaningfully different courses. A
state transition instead earns its MEET by reconciling several persistent
facts. Multiple Rounds arise when commitment changes but does not close the
situation. The **particularity test** asks whether the content would remain
substantially unchanged with another species, place, time, or condition; a
strong MEET makes at least one of those facts matter to response, cost, or
consequence.

### Names and animal language

Names translate a community's relationship with its world through concrete
animal-scale perceptions of material, enclosure, openness, height, route,
sound, weather, use, and history. Shared Practices keep their functional
names; proper names belong to particular Colonies, Reaches, Nodes, landmarks,
and remembered places. Curated banks provide speakable components rather than
procedural laws.

Mouse tends to notice edges, interiors, joins, and shelter; Rabbit shared
ground, breadth, neighborhood, and gathering; Squirrel height, anchors, lines,
direction, and reach. These are tendencies, not exclusive vocabularies.
Species voice likewise changes attention and comparison rather than facts,
intelligence, accent, or rigid syntax. Chronicle, Tales, dialogue, and scenes
may express the same world through those cultural perceptions. Familiar human
objects acquire ordinary shared terms; the animals do not rediscover fences,
drains, vehicles, and structures on each appearance.

Every individual Citizen receives a curated Given Name; a rarer After-name
arises from durable history. Collective Citizen forms use their approved
household names. Guest headings and rules use canonical short species names,
with longer established forms available in prose. Hyphen-bound forms mark only
deliberate animal-language concepts whose translation requires several English
elements. Appendix B supplies the full language and naming reference.

### Narrative throughout the Concept Sourcebook

Narrative makes time, relationship, particularity, decision, consequence, or
remembered change legible beside authoritative specification:

1. **Sustained narrative** carries a sequence that depends on continuity,
   especially the Founding Escape and potentially one continuous expedition.
2. **Recurring lived moments** revisit established Citizens, Places, objects,
   and relationships so familiarity can accumulate.
3. **Worked play sequences** follow concrete state through judgment,
   resolution, and persistent change.
4. **Literal game presentations** reproduce forms such as Chronicle, Tale,
   Almanac, MEET, RISK, and Community Board when the form itself is taught.
5. **Narrative-bearing art and environment** use Landscape Voice, captions,
   recurring geography, visual continuity, and established physical detail.

Part openings choose the form appropriate to their transition rather than one
formula. A full plate may carry one or two sentences of Landscape Voice; an
opening may instead use sustained narrative, a recurring Citizen, a literal
game presentation, or silence. Narrative sits beside the system it clarifies.
Tables, diagrams, and infographics carry fixed relationships; narrative earns
space through bodily scale, uncertainty, sequence, recognition, relationship,
departure, return, or accumulated history.

Ordinary work, proximity, care, hospitality, rest, observation, disagreement,
and routine establish what danger can change. Rabbit's prominence in the
Founding Escape is balanced by strong Mouse and Squirrel presence across the
book. The Augments Phase will assign narrative, artwork, diagrams, and game
presentations page by page using briefs that state governing section, function,
participants, place and time, incoming and outgoing state, and reason for
inclusion.

### Canon and precision

The GDD states positive canon: what exists, what the player does, which rule
applies, and what consequence persists. Intros establish purpose and
experience before qualifications. Removed mechanics disappear from active
specification; their history belongs to provenance.

Examples make rules concrete but bind only the propositions explicitly adopted
in canonical prose. Concept images and sample passages remain illustrative
until authorial review states an adopted proposition in its owning section.

Numbers appear when they create a legible decision, preserve a meaningful
ratio, or give a bounded system necessary exactness. Stable player-reasonable
relationships may use formulas; qualitative states carry differences that do
not benefit from arithmetic. Whole units, short ranges, and memorable
fractions predominate. Examples use the least precision needed, and unsettled
costs, chances, durations, or thresholds remain identified as tuning.

Where useful, local prose distinguishes:

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Canonical** | Approved v0.5 design in its owning section. |
| **Provisional** | Approved direction with identified detail still subject to a later pass. |
| **Tuning** | Quantity or balance to test without reopening the system's purpose. |
| **Open** | Unresolved design awaiting authorial judgment. |

These states create no second registry or lifecycle. An open matter remains
open; a quiet gap implies no mechanic.

### Landscape Orientation

**Landscape Orientation** turns a physical location into MEDIAN terrain,
composition, narration, and situated play:

1. Establish governing specification and world state.
2. Construct an exact cross-section: grade, material, vegetation, drainage,
   light, weather, sound, visibility, and human infrastructure.
3. Choose an animal-scale observation point.
4. Determine what is visible, concealed, reachable, useful, dangerous,
   familiar, and unknown from there.
5. Read those facts through the bodies, species, purposes, and histories
   present.
6. Derive candidate movement, choices, situations, images, and Landscape Voice.
7. Check each against its governing specification and obtain authority for any
   addition or exception.

One physical world supports several bodily readings. Terrain shapes
perception, choice, movement, and consequence. Art and prose may reveal
candidate affordances, but remain illustrative until accepted; established
systems and persistent state retain authority.

## 7.4 v0.5 scope and explicit deferrals

MEDIAN v0.5.0 is a Concept Sourcebook for a theoretical game. It establishes
the attachment-forward proposition; one persistent Home-and-Away world; five
Registers; Corridor, Colony, Citizen, expedition, decision, progression,
memory, interface, art, sound, and authoring systems; and the relationships
that make them one coherent work. It is complete as a concept that can be
imagined, examined, and developed further, not as a software-production plan.

### Dedicated Items and Resources tuning phase

A later conceptual pass will tune acquisition, Carry, Jostle, expedition
duration and drain, production, preservation, consumption, construction, and
other recurring costs as one economy. It will set the material and Civic
Share-Day requirements for Places, improvements, Tools, Supplies, Outposts,
restoration, and Civic Dedications against the expeditions and domestic
opportunity costs needed to provide them. It will test Core-Species Carry and
travel differences while preserving civic parity and resolve provisional
Supply-class names. Quantities change; adopted system relationships remain.

Other bounded tuning includes population and maturation timing, housing
multipliers, World Pressure cadence and Telegraphs, season and weather timing,
traffic thresholds, Node renewal, and marked chances, ranges, durations, or
frequencies.

### Later content and work

The first draft establishes representative MEETs, Places, Nodes, improvements,
histories, names, Tales, Chronicle selections, Landscape Voice, and visual
setups. Later authoring can expand those banks; a Practice receives another
improvement only when a real capability warrants it.

Appendix development precedes the Augments Phase, which will map narrative,
tables, diagrams, infographics, literal game presentations, illustration, and
full plates across the unified book without reopening the systems they explain.
**Family** remains provisional terminology for the relationship in Section
3.7; other explicitly open questions remain open in their owning sections.

The desktop and console computer game is the primary full-spectrum
manifestation. Tabletop roleplaying, cooperative fixed-content card, and
standalone mobile forms are translations that may receive their own concept
books. Later-version ideas remain evidence for future work until explicitly
brought into scope.

## 7.5 Canonical summary

MEDIAN is an attachment-forward animal-colony base builder set within the
median strips of an active modern highway. The player stewards one permanent
Home whose safety, beauty, capability, and history arise from a small
population of named Citizens. Sanctuary is achieved through credible Game
Logic, and its deepest reward is the successful stillness in which the player
can notice the Colony as a place worth caring about.

The game holds one persistent world across Home and Away. DWELL builds and
sustains the Colony; TRAVEL directly guides one party through the Field; RISK
expresses Crossing differently for Mouse, Rabbit, and Squirrel; MEET presents
situated decisions and consequential transitions; EMBODY lets the player live
as or be with Citizens when Quiet Equilibrium releases attention from urgent
stewardship, ordinarily through one small-cast invitation and exceptionally
through Open Table's collective Presence.

Home grows through Builder-designated Places, Residences, Practices, Projects,
and Citizen Civic Shares. Roles answer current Load; Readiness reveals civic
ability to answer it, and uncovered Load becomes Pressure. Completed Practices
provide durable strength while Role support enables their fullest civic and
productive use. Placement makes species preference spatially meaningful, and
Projects turn chosen shares toward lasting change.

Caretaker joins the care of lives through Hearth with the care of pooled Stores
through Storehouse. Storehouses make resources physically legible without
