## Appendix J — Manifestations: MEDIAN in Other Forms

`Philosophy.Pillar.AttachmentForward` · `Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

A **Manifestation** is an authored expression of MEDIAN in another form. The
desktop and console computer game remains the primary Manifestation and the
subject of this Concept Sourcebook. The possibilities below identify what
MEDIAN might offer other media without proposing ports of the primary game or
promising additional products.

### Translation principle

`Philosophy.Pillar.AttachmentForward` · `Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

MEDIAN translates through its world, relationships, and emotional direction
rather than through feature count. Another form may choose its own scale,
procedures, pacing, and player position while drawing upon the same narrow
animal country, active human Roads, particular Citizens, accumulated Home, and
movement between sanctuary and exposure.

> **A Manifestation succeeds when the player recognizes the same sanctuary,
> the same exposure, and the same particular friends even when almost none of
> the controls are shared.**

The briefs below describe promising directions rather than specifications.
Each would require its own design and prototype if ever pursued.

### Tabletop role-playing game

`Citizen.Citizen` · `Narrative.Memory`

MEDIAN offers a role-playing group one shared Home and a small community of
animals whose relationships, journeys, injuries, and memories can accumulate
across a campaign. Spoken play could bring exceptional depth to animal
perspectives, Colony life, Guest encounters, and the question of what is worth
risking beyond sanctuary. Its promise lies in collective storytelling around
particular lives rather than reproduction of the computer game’s management
systems.

> **The adventure is important because someone must carry it Home.**

### Spatial board game

`Home.Colony` · `Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

MEDIAN naturally offers a striking physical object: one animal Colony built on
a narrow strip between two active Roads. Players could watch named pieces leave
visible absences, construct a species-shaped Home together, and preserve the
marks of earlier sessions on the board. Its promise lies in making constrained
space, shared stewardship, and attachment to a physically accumulated place
tangible.

The defining image is a table where players can point to a patched shelter and
remember who built it, what crossed the Road to complete it, and who once lived
there.

### Cooperative fixed-content card game

`Architecture.Register.Encounter`

MEDIAN’s Citizens, relationships, objects, journeys, and consequences are well
suited to cards that recur and acquire meaning through association. A Citizen’s
departure could literally alter the Colony deck; a returned object or lasting
injury could remain in circulation for the rest of the campaign. Its promise
lies in making presence, absence, and memory structural within one complete
authored set.

> **Every Citizen changes the deck simply by being present.**

### Standalone mobile game

`Home.Embodiment`

A portrait phone screen already resembles a small median held between two
Roads. MEDIAN could become an intimate game of brief, voluntary visits to a
persistent Colony: noticing familiar Citizens, accompanying one small event,
and watching a particular Home change over time. Its promise lies in
pocket-scale familiarity and return, protected from streaks, absence penalties,
and emotional coercion.

> **The Colony waits without demanding.**

The following concept is the sole in-depth example. It demonstrates how a
Manifestation might embrace its medium and MEDIAN’s themes without
transliterating the primary game’s mechanics. Its detail gives the other forms
neither lesser status nor an implied production order.

### In-depth example — Standalone mobile game: *MEDIAN: One Small Place*

`Home.Embodiment`

#### Pitch

An intimate Colony chronicle played through short, deliberate visits to a
living median.

The portrait screen becomes the world’s geography. Home occupies a narrow
vertical strip through its center while traffic moves continuously along both
edges. The player holds the Colony between the Roads in their own hand.

This is a slow game about noticing a particular place often enough for it to
become familiar.

> **Each visit lets the player notice one thing, accompany one life, and leave
> one mark.**

#### Voluntary return

A visit lasts roughly five to eight minutes, although the player may remain and
observe for as long as desired. Time advances only when the player deliberately
turns the next Page. Closing the application leaves the Colony safe at its
current moment.

No Citizen becomes hungry because the player was busy. No project finishes
through idle accumulation. No notification summons the player back.

#### Home as a vertical living illustration

The main screen opens directly onto Home. The central median contains:

- visible shelters and familiar Places;
- moving, individually recognizable Citizens;
- plants changing with the season;
- objects retained from earlier events;
- traces of damage and repair;
- visiting animals;
- memorials and alterations; and
- ordinary activity.

Traffic at the screen edges supplies continual movement, light, sound, wake,
rain, litter, and disturbance. It makes the narrow safety of the center
perceptible without requiring constant emergencies.

The player may slide through the length of Home, pinch closer, or hold on a
location to listen and watch. The same doorway, gathering ground, Garden,
overlook, or patched roof appears repeatedly under different conditions.

#### Invitations rather than tasks

Each Page contains several subtle Invitations. The game does not immediately
list them.

A Citizen may pause near something unfinished. Grass may move where no resident
is visible. Someone may be setting out an extra portion. A familiar object may
be missing. An animal may repeatedly look toward the Road. The soundscape may
reveal something the image does not.

Touching and holding an Invitation opens its moment.

The player may discover several but follow only one before turning the Page:

- help with a part of Home;
- accompany a Citizen;
- welcome or observe a stranger;
- investigate a change;
- spend time within an ordinary routine;
- prepare for something beyond Home; or
- revisit something remembered.

Unchosen Invitations are not failed quests. Some recur, some develop
independently, and some pass. The Colony’s identity emerges partly from what
the player repeatedly chooses to notice.

#### One lasting mark

Most visits culminate in one small, persistent change:

- an object is placed;
- material becomes part of a shelter;
- two Citizens establish a routine;
- a Guest becomes familiar;
- someone learns a capability;
- a wound alters movement;
- a Citizen begins using a different Place;
- an absence becomes recognized; or
- a memory attaches to something visible.

The player sees the change rather than receiving a reward summary. A new strip
of fabric remains on the roof. Two animals begin sitting together at dusk. A
returning Citizen favors one leg. An object changes hands.

Many marks provide no mechanical advantage. They make this Colony increasingly
unlike every other one.

#### Citizens in the hand

Touching a Citizen opens a compact portrait with three layers:

- **Now** — where they are and what presently occupies them;
- **Familiar** — established habits, relationships, objects, and Places; and
- **Remembered** — selected moments that changed how the Colony knows them.

There are no affection bars. Repeated attention does not compel friendship or
manufacture intimacy through a meter. Familiarity grows because the player
recognizes continuity across appearances.

The player may follow different Citizens on different Pages. The Colony
remains the continuing subject; individual Citizens provide the lives through
which it becomes known.

#### Accompanying a Citizen

Some Invitations lead beyond Home. The presentation becomes a sequence of tall
illustrated spaces through which the player moves upward with the Citizen.

A journey is a chain of embodied moments:

- moving beneath wet grass;
- listening beside a Sound Wall;
- waiting under a discarded container;
- encountering another animal;
- deciding whether to continue into unfamiliar ground;
- carrying something awkward; or
- finding a different way back.

The player advances by touch, pause, observation, and occasional directional
choice. The sequence can last several Pages, leaving that Citizen visibly
absent from Home until their return.

#### The phone turns when the world does

Home is held in portrait orientation: a narrow sanctuary between two moving
edges.

When a Citizen attempts a Roadway Crossing, the player rotates the phone into
landscape orientation. The Road suddenly fills the screen.

Traffic passes laterally through image, sound, and haptic vibration. The player
watches for movement, feels approaching weight, and uses a small number of
deliberate gestures to move, shelter, wait, or retreat. The Crossing lasts less
than a minute, but the physical reorientation makes it feel like entering a
different and overwhelming world.

After reaching the opposite Margin or returning Home, the player rotates the
phone upright. The median becomes visible again.

Accessible visual, audio, and simplified-control alternatives preserve the
same dramatic transformation without requiring sound, vibration, precise
timing, or physical rotation.

#### Species transforms the phone

Each Core Species reorganizes the same portrait device.

##### Mouse — joined detail

Mouse Home becomes a dense sequence of linked interiors and adjacent spaces.
The player moves through it by slipping between close views rather than
surveying broad ground. Several Mice are often visible together, and an
accompanied moment may involve a pair whose small differences gradually become
recognizable.

##### Rabbit — open center

Rabbit Home preserves a broad vertical commons through the center of the
screen. Citizens cross one another’s paths visibly and gather in changing
groups. Away movement covers long stretches through fluid upward gestures,
while Crossing emphasizes decisive bursts.

##### Squirrel — vertical connection

Squirrel Home climbs the screen. Bridges, branches, supports, and hanging
Places create several elevations inside the portrait frame. The player follows
connections upward and downward, with the device naturally becoming a view
into the Web.

Species changes what the player’s thumb learns to do and what kind of space
becomes familiar.

#### Quiet Pages

Some Pages contain no consequential Invitation.

The player may watch a meal, follow a Citizen’s ordinary route, listen to rain
under shelter, find animals sleeping in unexpected arrangements, or remain
with the changing sound of evening traffic.

A **Stay** gesture delays the next Page for as long as the player wishes.
Nothing is earned for staying, and nothing is lost by moving on.

Quiet is part of the game’s content rather than an interval between rewards.

#### Illustrated memory

Every lasting mark produces a visual amendment to the Colony or a Citizen’s
current image. At the end of a season, the game assembles selected Pages into
an illustrated vertical Chronicle.

The player can scroll backward through:

- earlier forms of Home;
- arrivals and departures;
- recovered objects;
- relationships becoming visible;
- dangerous Crossings;
- ordinary repeated moments;
- seasonal transformations; and
- Citizens who are no longer present.

The Chronicle is generated from established play. The player does not select
flattering highlights or write captions to prove that something mattered.

Earlier images remain available, but ordinary play always opens on the Colony
as it exists now.

#### Campaign shape

A complete first campaign might contain sixty to one hundred Pages, experienced
whenever the player chooses. It follows one Colony through a year of
settlement, relationships, departures, Guests, weather, and change.

Completion produces a final current portrait and a readable seasonal
Chronicle. The player may continue into another year, close this Colony’s book,
or begin again with another Core Species.

A five-minute visit remains meaningful because it belongs to a history
accumulated over weeks or months of voluntary return.

#### Characteristic visit

The player opens the game after ten days away. No time penalty has accumulated.
Home resumes at a rainy morning Page.

Holding on the Storehouse reveals that its roof is dripping. Holding on a young
Citizen reveals that she is watching a Groundhog Guest preparing to leave.
Holding near the Road reveals a bright piece of waterproof material caught
against the verge.

The player follows the young Citizen and the Groundhog toward it. At the
Roadway, the phone rotates. They wait through the vibration of two passing
trucks, retrieve the material, and return.

Back in portrait view, the player places it over the Storehouse. The Page closes
on the two animals sheltering beneath the newly patched roof while rain
continues along both Roads.

Weeks later, the same blue material remains visible.

#### Product ethic

The game is sold as a complete work. Its return rhythm comes from attachment
rather than pressure:

- no streaks;
- no expiring daily rewards;
- no deterioration during absence;
- no push notification claiming that a Citizen needs help;
- no premium currency;
- no randomized purchases; and
- no payments attached to recovery, survival, or affection.

Optional notifications may announce only a reminder deliberately requested by
the player. The Colony never impersonates emotional distress to compel
engagement.

#### Form-native thesis

The design uses the device itself as part of MEDIAN’s meaning:

- the portrait screen resembles the long, narrow territory of a median;
- the player physically holds Home between two Roads;
- touch encourages attention to small movement and detail;
- rotation lets the Roadway overwhelm the device during Crossing;
- sound and haptics make human traffic physically present;
- brief Pages support a relationship built through recurring voluntary visits;
  and
- the image archive allows a Colony to remain literally carried in the
  player’s pocket.

> **The Colony waits without demanding. The player returns because it has
> become somewhere worth visiting.**

Time advances only through player-advanced Pages. Real-world light or season
may gently recolor presentation, but absence never advances needs, ends
opportunities, or harms Citizens. This protects the difference between
attachment and coercion.

### Manifestation boundary

`Philosophy.Pillar.AttachmentForward` · `Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

The four forms above are creative possibilities rather than announced
products, canonical rule translations, or production commitments. The mobile
example demonstrates a method: begin with what MEDIAN makes promising in a
medium, then design an original game around that promise. Any future
Manifestation would earn its own specification only through separate authorial
development and prototyping.

## Appendix K — Market Position and Comparative Landscape

`Philosophy.Philosophy` · `Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

MEDIAN occupies two related positions. The theoretical desktop and console game
has a comparative field among colony simulations, settlement strategy,
character-driven games, animal-scale adventures, and ecological fiction. The
artifact that presently exists is an illustrated Concept Sourcebook whose
comparative field includes videogame design books, role-playing sourcebooks,
illustrated worldbooks, fictional field guides, and game-design fiction.

These positions must remain distinct. The Concept Sourcebook is the completed
present artifact. Its achievement is to make the theoretical game and world
sufficiently coherent, legible, and inhabitable that the book provides a
satisfying experience in its own right.

This Appendix establishes durable comparative relationships and positioning
language. Statements about projects currently released or in development are
contained in a visibly dated account and require fresh verification whenever
they are used outside this edition.

### Positioning the theoretical game

`Philosophy.Philosophy` · `Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

MEDIAN's clearest position arises from the relationship among Home, departure,
exposure, consequence, and return.

> **MEDIAN is an attachment-forward animal-colony strategy game about building
> one permanent Home in the median of a living highway, then deciding which
> named Citizens must leave its safety, cross a Roadway, and return changed.**

This statement gives the reader concrete subjects and actions before asking an
authored design term to carry meaning. **Attachment-forward** names the design
direction developed in § 1.2 and Appendix I, but it is not an established
market genre and should always be explained through what the game lets the
player know, value, remember, and protect.

| Positioning level | Preferred language | Function |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Public lead | Attachment-forward animal-colony strategy | Names the subject and design direction without implying an industrial city builder. |
| Formal genre | Ecological colony-and-expedition strategy game | Locates the complete Home-and-Away structure. |
| Secondary description | Narrative colony simulation | Signals named Citizens, persistent consequence, and Campaign Memory. |
| Milieu | Animal civilization in a living highway median | Establishes the defining territory in direct language. |
| Dramatic rhythm | Sanctuary → departure → exposure → consequence → return | States the recurring emotional and mechanical movement. |
| Emotional proposition | **MEDIAN earns its cozy.** | Places warmth as an achieved and particular condition rather than a universal guarantee. |

The formal genre label is useful after the concrete proposition is understood.
Used alone, *ecological colony-and-expedition strategy game* can sound more
abstract than MEDIAN feels and can conceal the named animals whose lives give
the systems weight.

### Cumulative distinctiveness

`Philosophy.Philosophy` · `Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

MEDIAN's position is cumulative. Each element has useful comparisons
elsewhere; their interdependence forms the proposition.

| Layer | MEDIAN's expression |
| --- | --- |
| Territory | A permanent animal civilization within the active infrastructure of a contemporary highway rather than a generic woodland or abandoned human ruin. |
| Home | One irreplaceable founding Colony whose Places, repairs, images, relationships, and memories accumulate across the campaign. |
| Citizens | A small named population whose members remain historically continuous and function as fractional player characters rather than anonymous labor units. |
| Species | Mouse, Rabbit, and Squirrel transform body scale, settlement form, Carry, Field movement, RISK, and the campaign's felt character. |
| Modes and Registers | One persistent world expressed through Home and Away, and through DWELL, TRAVEL, RISK, MEET, and EMBODY. |
| Departure and return | Particular Citizens leave visible absences, undergo exposure and consequence, and bring changed bodies, relationships, materials, and memories Home. |
| Civic life | Roles, Practices, Civic Balance, and the Project Queue create a non-industrial language of shared responsibility. |
| Sufficiency | Quiet Equilibrium makes stability, ordinary life, and successful stillness genuine outcomes rather than pauses before mandatory expansion. |
| Memory | Record, Chronicle, Tale, and canon imagery allow history to alter how Citizens, objects, Places, and the Colony are understood. |

The active highway is the immediate discovery hook. It must appear before
generic woodland-animal imagery can determine the comparison. The permanent
Home and its named lives then reveal the deeper proposition.

> **The highway makes MEDIAN recognizable. Return makes it matter.**

### Audience intersection

`Philosophy.Pillar.AttachmentForward`

MEDIAN brings several existing audiences into one work:

- settlement and colony-strategy players who value legible growth but want a
  destination other than industrial scale or perpetual optimization;
- players who remember particular simulated characters, injuries,
  relationships, objects, and emergent histories;
- readers of serious animal-scale fiction in which bodies and species remain
  consequential;
- players interested in ecological and environmental storytelling grounded in
  active human infrastructure;
- readers who value illustrated worlds, sourcebooks, maps, diagrams, and
  systems as cultural objects; and
- readers interested in game design as an expressive form even when no
  executable product accompanies the design.

Each audience supplies a recognizable point of entry. Their intersection
preserves the complete relationship among Colony, Citizen, Roadway, and
return.

### Comparative method

`Philosophy.Philosophy`

Comparison serves several different purposes. Treating every neighboring work
as a competitor or every resemblance as an influence obscures more than it
clarifies.

| Class | Question |
| --- | --- |
| Current market neighbor | What might a contemporary reader or player compare from a title, store description, trailer, or screenshot? |
| Structural ancestor | What earlier work demonstrates a useful part of MEDIAN's game architecture? |
| Audience or tonal neighbor | What work attracts a related audience or creates a related emotional expectation despite different mechanics? |
| Milieu relative | What work shares animal scale, human infrastructure, road danger, refuge, or ecological attention? |
| Creative influence | What work actually shaped MEDIAN's authorial development? |
| Counter-comparable | What nearby design makes an illuminating opposite choice? |

A comparison establishes neither descent nor equivalence. Public positioning
should acknowledge meaningful overlap, state the boundary concretely, and
avoid absolute originality claims. The reviewed field distributes MEDIAN's
closest comparisons across several works.

### Current market field — verified 15 September 2026

`Philosophy.Philosophy`

This subsection records a changing field. Release state, feature descriptions,
and project direction must be verified again before reuse.

| Work | Why the comparison arises | MEDIAN's boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [*Whiskerwood*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/2489330/Whiskerwood/) | The strongest screenshot and keyword collision: mice, elaborate settlements, individual colonists, survival, and expeditions. It entered Early Access on 6 November 2025 and is now an encountered product rather than a hypothetical future neighbor. | Its official proposition foregrounds complex production chains, automation, commodities, vertical industrial cities, cat oppression, and eventual rebellion. MEDIAN's Colony develops so that particular lives and their returns can matter; production remains bounded to that civic purpose. |
| [*Croakwood*](https://www.croakwood.com/) | Tiny animal townsfolk, visible daily routines, functional roles, customizable construction, exploration, and a relaxed woodland settlement make this the most important addition to the earlier survey. | Its presented center is frog town-building and management through gathering, crafting, decoration, and expansion into the forest. MEDIAN centers a small historical roster, an active Highway, distinct Home and Away responsibilities, and consequential return. |
| [*Luminids*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/4070510/Luminids/) | Its overhead colony planning and first-person entry into the built settlement create the strongest presentation-level comparison to a change of Register. Its announced Early Access date is February 2027. | Its public promise is an open colony sandbox without combat or game-over pressure. EMBODY is a bounded Home Register opened through Quiet Equilibrium, while TRAVEL and RISK carry animal bodies into credible danger. |
| [*Winter Burrow*](https://noodlecake.com/games/winter-burrow/) | A mouse restores a childhood Home, gathers beyond it, meets neighbors, and moves between warmth and a dangerous winter exterior. It offers the clearest current tonal-audience overlap. | One embodied protagonist drives an approachable survival adventure. MEDIAN's continuing subject is a Colony whose many Citizens can become individually known. |
| [*Hawthorn*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/4787870/Hawthorn_ProofofConcept_Demo/) | Anthropomorphic village life, memorable neighbors, building, farming, craft, exploration, seasons, and optional danger share a broad audience promise. | Its proof-of-concept presents an avatar-led sandbox role-playing experience. MEDIAN maintains collective stewardship and only enters individual company through the bounded EMBODY Register. |
| [*The Wandering Village*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/1121640/The_Wandering_Village/) | Constrained settlement space, hostile beauty, ecological dependence, changing conditions, and outward scouting provide a strong colony-plus-environment comparison. | Its settlement travels on a living creature and foregrounds production-chain adaptation. MEDIAN's founding Home remains fixed, and Away gains meaning through return to it. |

Several additional works create narrower points of comparison. *Ratopia* joins
animal city-building, individually differentiated citizens, policy, and
expeditions to an explicit economy of wealth, taxation, labor, and urban
prosperity. *Timberborn* joins species-shaped construction, verticality,
ecological hazard, and scavenged human material to automation, engineering,
terraforming, and mechanical production. *Grimshire* joins anthropomorphic
community, seasonal preservation, projects, and possible loss to a
player-character farming and life simulation. *Small Saga* and *Rain World*
offer especially useful animal-scale treatments of human infrastructure,
politics, vulnerability, and ecological danger while pursuing heroic or
nomadic forms far from MEDIAN's permanent civic Home.

The marketplace therefore produces several comparisons at once. Whiskerwood
may supply the first image comparison, Croakwood the animal-town comparison,
Luminids the viewpoint comparison, Winter Burrow the emotional comparison,
and The Wandering Village the colony-and-environment comparison. Positioning
should welcome this plurality rather than force one title to explain the whole
design.

### Structural ancestors and counter-comparables

`Architecture.Cycle.HomeAwayReturn`

The strongest mechanical lineage often lies outside animal games.

| Work | Durable contribution to comparison | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [*This War of Mine*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/282070/This_War_of_Mine/) | The clearest broad predecessor for a shelter maintained at Home while particular civilians leave it, face danger, and return with resources and consequence. | Its dramatic engine is continuing civilian desperation. MEDIAN permits civic continuity, growth, care, ordinary life, and successful stillness. |
| [*RimWorld*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/294100/RimWorld/) | A definitive precedent for attachment through simulated needs, relationships, wounds, environmental events, travel, and strange individual history. | Its possibility space, body granularity, combat, psychology, and production systems are deliberately broader. MEDIAN uses a smaller authored grammar and a consciously bounded population. |
| [*Clanfolk*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/1700870/Clanfolk/) | One inhabited homestead, individual lives, family continuity, Guests, seasons, work, and preparation for winter make it an important Home-side neighbor. | Skills, lineage, labor, and production optimization organize its multigenerational survival. MEDIAN's Body Units, Civic Shares, Roles, and Campaign Memory pursue civic parity and attachment through different abstractions. |
| [*Wildermyth*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/763890/Wildermyth/) | Characters accumulate relationships, transformations, injury, age, sacrifice, retirement, memory, and later reappearance. | Its center is a heroic tactical party becoming legend. MEDIAN's Citizens become histories because they belong to a continuing Colony. |
| [*Against the Storm*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/1336490/Against_the_Storm/) | Multi-species settlement strategy, environmental Pressure, recurring events, expeditions, and strong city-building legibility make it an illuminating counter-comparable. | Its roguelite structure repeatedly founds, completes, and relinquishes settlements. MEDIAN makes one Home irreplaceable. |
| [*Of Life and Land*](https://store.steampowered.com/app/1733110/Of_Life_and_Land/) | Interdependent plants, animals, citizens, climate, and settlement activity offer a useful contemporary ecological-simulation comparison. | MEDIAN uses a selective ecological model in which relationships shape animal-scale territory, species expression, Pressure, opportunity, and consequence. |

These works clarify separate parts of MEDIAN: *This War of Mine* clarifies
departure and return; *RimWorld* and *Clanfolk* clarify attachment to an
inhabited settlement; *Wildermyth* clarifies accumulated personal history;
*Against the Storm* clarifies permanence by choosing its opposite.

### Creative lineage and market shorthand

`Philosophy.Pillar.AttachmentForward`

MEDIAN states its creative lineage directly in Part I. *Watership Down*
supplies its deepest emotional and dramaturgical foundation; *Mouse Guard*
informs animal-scale material culture, serious adventure, and sourcebook form;
and *Age of Empires* supplies the formative pleasure of legible civic growth.

Those relationships should remain honest influences rather than public
equations. “*Watership Down* meets *Age of Empires*” hides the distinctions
the Concept Sourcebook has worked to make: collective stewardship without
warfare, animal bodies without borrowed societies, and construction serving
attachment rather than technological ascent. Creative ancestry deserves
acknowledgment; it cannot substitute for describing the present design.

### Positioning the Concept Sourcebook

`Philosophy.Philosophy`

The Concept Sourcebook is the present work. It wraps the complete v0.5.0 game
design in a reader-facing visual and narrative form. Its rules, maps, tables,
prototypes, story Colonies, canon images, diagrams, and prose cooperate to make
the theoretical game available to imagination.

Nearby book forms each contribute part of its reading grammar:

| Book form | Useful precedent | MEDIAN's position |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Videogame art and design book | [*Sable: Design Works*](https://www.lostincult.co.uk/shop/p/sable-video-game-art-book-deluxe) and related design volumes demonstrate sustained visual authorship, annotated concepts, maps, development analysis, and highly designed physical presentation. | These books look backward from a completed game. MEDIAN's book is the primary completed expression of a theoretical one. |
| Illustrated worldbook or novel | [*Tales from the Loop*](https://designstudiopress.com/products/tales-from-the-loop), [*The Electric State*](https://www.simonandschuster.co.uk/books/The-Electric-State/Simon-Stalenhag/9781471176081), and *Dinotopia* demonstrate that image, prose, and a coherent world can constitute the principal experience. | MEDIAN also exposes rules, interfaces, state, choice, and systemic consequence. The imagined game is part of the world being made present. |
| Role-playing sourcebook or fictional field guide | [*A Field Guide to Hot Springs Island*](https://shop.swordfishislands.com/a-field-guide-to-hot-springs-island/) demonstrates the pleasure of a fictional territory made concrete and navigable through reference form. | MEDIAN borrows sourcebook density and navigation while describing a theoretical computer game rather than supplementing a playable tabletop system. |
| Fictional game manual and game-design fiction | [*Q1: Void*](https://droqen.itch.io/q1-void) demonstrates the imaginative experience of encountering a videogame through a manual that is itself the released work. | MEDIAN pursues full systemic and world coherence rather than nostalgic fragmentation or the deliberate mystery of a missing game. |

The resulting category is unusual but legible:

> **An illustrated Concept Sourcebook for a complete theoretical game, in
> which rules, world, narrative, interface, and artwork together make the game
> inhabitable in the reader's imagination.**

The term **Concept Sourcebook** therefore requires a short definition wherever
it first appears. It names a reader-facing theoretical design object whose
scope is complete without a software-production promise. The book is complete
when the reader can understand MEDIAN's Game Logic, imagine its lived play,
move confidently between overview and detail, and experience the world as more
than a collection of attractive images.

Several production choices protect that position:

- state the book's theoretical scope early;
- design every page while allowing many pages to rely on typography, tables,
  diagrams, and white space rather than illustration;
- remake useful visual concepts against current canon instead of treating
  obsolete artwork as authority;
- let narrative demonstrate systems without converting the whole book into
  fiction;
- preserve exact reference depth where it gives the imagined game credibility;
- use prototype images to communicate the Registers while identifying them as
  presentational illustrations; and
- make the book satisfying without implying that software realization is its
  deferred ending.

### Resemblance and misreading risks

`Philosophy.Pillar.AttachmentForward`

The greatest risk is convergence toward familiar neighbors in pursuit of
immediate legibility.

| Likely reading | Why it arises | Positioning protection |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “A cozy animal city builder” | Warm art, small dwellings, gardens, craft, and animal Citizens are immediately visible. | Show the active Highway, a named low population, departure, RISK, and Homecoming before generic settlement abundance determines the promise. |
| “Whiskerwood without cats” | Isometric rodent architecture creates an unavoidable first-image resemblance. | Preserve all three Core Species, non-industrial civic scale, the fixed median territory, and the Home/Away rhythm. |
| “A colony game with a first-person mode” | EMBODY can be mistaken for a camera feature comparable to Luminids. | Present EMBODY last among the Registers and explain Quiet Equilibrium, chosen company, bounded Home experiences, and attachment as its purpose. |
| “A RimWorld-like story generator” | Named Citizens, wounds, relationships, events, and persistent history suggest unrestricted simulation. | Describe the authored MEET grammar, Campaign Memory, low population, fractional-PC conception, and Conservation of Systems. |
| “A *Watership Down* or *Mouse Guard* game” | The influences are real and visually legible. | State the lineage while foregrounding MEDIAN's modern highway ecology, civic structure, three equal Core civilizations, and distinct Game Logic. |
| “A climate or ecology simulation” | Road ecology, Traffic as Weather, Biomes, and animal bodies are prominent. | Show ecology through decisions, territory, species experience, Pressure, and consequence rather than promising exhaustive natural simulation. |
| “A pitch book for a future product” | Concept art and a theoretical game can resemble preproduction presentation. | Name the Concept Sourcebook as the completed present artifact and give its prose, reference systems, narrative, and visual architecture full reader-facing form. |

A useful comparison opens a door while MEDIAN's own proposition determines
what waits beyond it.

### Messaging sequence

`Philosophy.Philosophy`

Any concise public account should reveal MEDIAN in this order:

1. **The place** — a living median within an active modern highway.
2. **The subject** — one permanent Colony and its small population of named
   animal Citizens.
3. **The rhythm** — build sanctuary, choose who leaves, cross and explore, and
   return with consequence.
4. **The species** — Mouse, Rabbit, and Squirrel create materially different
   civilizations and campaigns.
5. **The reward** — Quiet Equilibrium and EMBODY make ordinary life available
   to attention.
6. **The horizon** — the Colony and its Citizens become historically specific
   through Campaign Memory.

The setting should appear before a generic animal settlement can establish the
image. Return should appear before exploration can be mistaken for an end in
itself. EMBODY should appear after the strategy game that makes its quiet
meaningful.

#### Recommended descriptions

**One sentence**

> MEDIAN is an attachment-forward animal-colony strategy game about building
> one permanent Home in the median of a living highway, then deciding which
> named Citizens must leave its safety, cross a Roadway, and return changed.

**Two sentences**

> Mice, Rabbits, or Squirrels build one permanent Colony between the
> carriageways of an active modern highway. The player sustains its civic life,
> guides named Citizens into the world beyond Home, and lives with everything
> they bring back: materials, relationships, injuries, obligations, and
> memory.

**Concept Sourcebook**

> MEDIAN is an illustrated Concept Sourcebook for a complete theoretical game:
> an attachment-forward animal Colony strategy built around sanctuary,
> exposure, consequence, and return.

#### Language discipline

Prefer concrete language such as **one permanent Home**, **named Citizens**,
**living highway**, **colony-and-expedition**, **animal-scale**, **return**,
**Quiet Equilibrium**, and **Campaign Memory**. Use **attachment-forward** with
its definition. Use **highway-riparian** as authored analytical language after
the ordinary physical setting is clear.

Use **cozy**, **city builder**, **base builder**, **RTS**, **story generator**,
and **first-person mode** only with the qualification each requires. Private
comparisons such as “*RimWorld* with animals” or “*Watership Down* meets *Age
of Empires*” may expose one useful relationship during development but should
not become public definitions.

### Positioning acceptance test

`Philosophy.Philosophy`

The position is legible when a reader can explain:

1. why the active highway median is territory rather than backdrop;
2. why one permanent Home matters more than repeated settlement;
3. why Citizens are particular lives rather than a labor population;
4. why Home, Away, and Homecoming form one continuous game;
5. how Mouse, Rabbit, and Squirrel create distinct campaign experiences;
6. why Quiet Equilibrium and EMBODY are rewards of competent stewardship;
7. why Whiskerwood, Croakwood, Luminids, This War of Mine, RimWorld, and
   Wildermyth are each useful comparisons but none describes the whole;
8. how attachment-forward differs from a promise of continuous coziness; and
9. why the Concept Sourcebook is a completed theoretical design object rather
   than an announcement of software production.

MEDIAN occupies crowded neighboring shelves in a combination the reviewed
field does not duplicate: colony strategy without industrial destiny; animal
vulnerability without solitary survival; road ecology within active human
infrastructure; named personal history without heroic-party primacy; and
sanctuary as the reason the other systems matter.

# END MATTER

Bibliography and endnotes, index, credits and colophon, version information,
and the End Plate remain distinct end matter rather than numbered Appendices.
